        Wait, how can the pawn at c7 move?
        It's at c7. It can move to c6 or c5.
        But there is already a white pawn at c6!
        So the black pawn at c7 is blocked by the white pawn at c6.
        This is why the Queen at d8 cannot move to b6 or a5.

        So, the Queen at d8 is limited.
        The most powerful move available is capturing the white queen.
        `d8d1`
        Even if it's a trade (Qxd1, Rxd1), it's a strong simplification.

        Wait, let me look at the white king again.
        Can we attack it with the bishop and queen?
        Bishop is at e6.
        Queen is at d8.
        Wait, what if we move the bishop to f5? (`e6f5`)
        Then the bishop controls the d3 square? No.
        What if we move the bishop to g4? (`e6g4`)
        Then the bishop controls the d1 square? No.

        Let's think about the rook at f8.
        f8-e8 (`f8e8`).
        Now the rook is at e8.
        Does it do anything? No.

        Let's think about the rook at a8.
        a8-b8 (`a8b8`).
        Now the rook is at b8.
        Now the b-file is controlled.

        Let's reconsider the `d8d1` move.
        Qxd1.
        White must respond.
        If White plays Rxd1, Black has successfully traded queens.
        Wait, is there any other response for White?
        White King is at c3.
        Can the King take d1?
        c3 to d1: 1 file, 2 ranks. No.
        Can any other piece take d1?
        Bf1? No.
        Rh1? No.
        So White *must* play Rxd1.

        Let's double check if there's anything better.
        Wait, what about `e6c4`?
        Bishop to c4.
        Now the bishop at c4 controls b3 and d3.
        And the King is at c3.
        Wait, if the bishop is at c4, he's attacking the king? No.
        But if Black then plays Qd8-d3+?
        If the bishop is at c4, it's NOT protecting d3. (Bishops move diagonally).
        Wait, c4 to d3 is a diagonal move!
        Yes! c4 to d3 is a diagonal move!
        (c-d is 1 file, 4-3 is 1 rank).
        So, if Black plays Be6-c4, and then played Qd8-d3+, the bishop at c4 would protect the queen at d3!
        Let's see:
        1. ... Be6-c4
        2. (White move)
        3. ... Qd8-d3+
        Now, if the White Queen at d1 takes the Black Queen at d3 (Qxd3), the Black Bishop at c4 takes back (Bxd3).
        This is also a queen trade.

        Is there any move for White that stops this?
        After 1... Be6-c4, White could move the king, move the queen, or attack the bishop.
        For example, White could play Kd3, attacking the bishop at c4.
        Then the bishop must move.

        Actually, the simplest and most direct move to trade queens (or win one) is `d8d1`.
        It's an immediate capture.
